Like my heart follow-up appointments, or... returning calls to the clinic. Sometimes I'll go to help someone with a repair job and realize I didn't bring the right tools. It's just... becoming a problem, I suppose.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these memory problems, and have they been getting worse over time?\n\nPatient: I suppose... maybe a year and two months. That's when it became a real trouble. \n\nI had a cardiac scare around then. Had to cut back on my walking... and the projects I usually do. They used to keep me settled, I guess. Since I had to stop all that, it's just been... like this.\n\nDoctor: Since your cardiac scare and the changes in your activity level, have you noticed feeling down, sad, or losing interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... I still like doing things. I just... can't do as much of them right now. I don't feel sad or anything. Just... frustrated sometimes, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself confused or unsure of where you are, like getting lost in familiar places?\n\nPatient: No... no, I haven't had anything like that. I know where I am. I just... forget the appointments, like I said.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep or appetite since you had to cut back on your usual activities?\n\nPatient: No... sleep's about the same. Appetite too. Haven't noticed any difference.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself getting more irritable or frustrated lately, even if you don't feel down or sad?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Just... what I mentioned before about not doing as much. But I wouldn't say I'm more irritable. I do alright with all that. Bills get paid. I remember things well enough. Just... normal everyday stuff, I handle it fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any days where you just don't take pleasure in things that usually make you happy, even if you don't feel sad?\n\nPatient: No... not really. If I like doing something, I still like doing it. Hasn't been an issue.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in how motivated you feel to start or finish tasks, even small ones, since your cardiac scare?\n\nPatient: No... I wouldn't say that. I still get up and do whatever needs doing. The heart thing didn't really change that. Motivation is fine, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any trouble organizing your thoughts, making decisions, or keeping track of multiple things at once since your heart scare?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, my thoughts are fine. I can make decisions just like before. Nothing's really changed there.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any trouble remembering things like recent events, conversations, or familiar information that you would normally have no problem recalling?\n\nPatient: No... memory's fine. I mean, I remember what I need to. Haven't noticed any trouble with that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel distressed or concerned about these memory problems, or do you feel like they’re interfering with your ability to live your life the way you want to?\n\nPatient: I don't know about distressed. It's just... well, I guess it's gotten to where the senior-center repair group can’t really count on me the way they used to. Just... frustrated sometimes, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself confused or unsure of where you are, like getting lost in familiar places?\n\nPatient: No... no, I haven't had anything like that. I know where I am. I just... forget the appointments, like I said.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep or appetite since you had to cut back on your usual activities?\n\nPatient: No... sleep's about the same. Appetite too. Haven't noticed any difference.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself getting more irritable or frustrated lately, even if you don't feel down or sad?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Just... what I mentioned before about not doing as much. But I wouldn't say I'm more irritable. Just normal, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any difficulty with handling everyday tasks that require thinking, like following a recipe, keeping track of bills, or remembering recent conversations?\n\nPatient: No... I do alright with all that. Bills get paid. I remember things well enough. Just... normal everyday stuff, I handle it fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any days where you just don't take pleasure in things that usually make you happy, even if you don't feel sad?\n\nPatient: No... not really. If I like doing something, I still like doing it. Hasn't been an issue.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in how motivated you feel to start or finish tasks, even small ones, since your cardiac scare?\n\nPatient: No... I wouldn't say that. I still get up and do whatever needs doing. The heart thing didn't really change that. Motivation is fine, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any trouble organizing your thoughts, making decisions, or keeping track of multiple things at once since your heart scare?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, my thoughts are fine. I can make decisions just like before. Nothing's really changed there.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any trouble remembering things like recent events, conversations, or familiar information that you would normally have no problem recalling?\n\nPatient: No... memory's fine. I mean, I remember what I need to. Haven't noticed any trouble with that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel distressed or concerned about these memory problems, or do you feel like they’re interfering with your ability to live your life the way you want to?\n\nPatient: I don't know about distressed. It's just... well, I guess it's gotten to where the senior-center repair group can’t really count on me the way they used to. And... my doctor’s office has had to chase me down about some missed heart follow-ups. I still get up and do whatever needs doing. The heart thing didn't really change that. Motivation is fine, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any trouble organizing your thoughts, making decisions, or keeping track of multiple things at once since your heart scare?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, my thoughts are fine. I can make decisions just like before. Nothing's really changed there.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any trouble remembering things like recent events, conversations, or familiar information that you would normally have no problem recalling?\n\nPatient: No... memory's fine. I mean, I remember what I need to. Haven't noticed any trouble with that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel distressed or concerned about these memory problems, or do you feel like they’re interfering with your ability to live your life the way you want to?\n\nPatient: I don't know about distressed. It's just... well, I guess it's gotten to where the senior-center repair group can’t really count on me the way they used to. And... my doctor’s office has had to chase me down about some missed heart follow-ups. That's about it.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me the date today, and who the current president is?\n\nPatient: It's Thursday. The 18th. And Biden is the president.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any days where you feel like you just don’t care as much about things as you used to, even if you’re not feeling down?\n\nPatient: No... I wouldn't say that. I still care about the things I usually do. Haven't really noticed any change like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're not as concerned about these memory problems as others seem to be, or do you feel like they're about as important as people make them out to be?\n\nPatient: I haven't really thought about it like that. I guess... no, I don't think they're making a bigger deal out of it than I am. It just is what it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself not really worrying about things as much as you used to, even if you know they should matter?\n\nPatient: Not really... I still worry about the usual stuff, I guess. It's about the same.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. If I like doing something, I still like doing it. Hasn't been an issue.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in how motivated you feel to start or finish tasks, even small ones, since your cardiac scare?\n\nPatient: No... I wouldn't say that. I still get up and do whatever needs doing. The heart thing didn't really change that. I mean, my thoughts are fine. I can make decisions just like before. I mean, I remember what I need to. I wouldn't say that. I still care about the things I usually do. Haven't really noticed any change like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're not as concerned about these memory problems as others seem to be, or do you feel like they're about as important as people make them out to be?\n\nPatient: I haven't really thought about it like that. I guess... no, I don't think they're making a bigger deal out of it than I am. It just is what it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself not really worrying about things as much as you used to, even if you know they should matter?\n\nPatient: Not really... I still worry about the usual stuff, I guess. It's about the same.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it harder than before to learn new information, like instructions for a new tool or medication, or figuring out how to do something you haven’t done before?\n\nPatient: No... I haven't forgotten how to do things around the house.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any problems getting along with others or handling your usual responsibilities at work or in your daily life because of memory or thinking issues?\n\nPatient: No...
